# milestone08

## index

- 001 — array: 2D array literal and nested indexing
- 002 — array: sized 2D matrix i32[N][M], zero-init and writes
- 003 — array: 3D sized array with runtime size
- 004 — array: heap array: new<i32>(N), zero-init, write, free
- 005 — array: heap array with a runtime size, new<i32>(n) + free
- 006 — array: multi-dim heap: new<i32[]>(N), a row each, free each
- 008 — array: arr.len on a literal and on multi-dim
- 012 — array: .len on a non-array
- 013 — array: array literals: one element, expressions, and strings
- 014 — array: fill a heap char buffer by index, print it, free it
- 015 — enum: a variant as an array index and in arithmetic
- 017 — heap: new<T>(1) gives one zeroed struct, a ref points at it
- 021 — heap: operator drop frees the nodes a tree owns
- 022 — heap: a function fills a heap struct and returns a ref to it
- 025 — lexer: an index with nothing in it
- 026 — lexer: :: must be followed by a name
- 027 — operator: operator drop declares a destructor named Vec.drop
- 029 — operator: calling .drop() yourself is rejected
- 030 — operator: operator drop beside operator = does not warn
- 031 — operator: a destructor fires on break and on continue
- 032 — operator: drop on fall-through: an if block, each iteration, reverse order
- 033 — operator: a struct drops the structs it owns, at any depth, but not what it borrows
- 034 — operator: a heap-owning struct: create, deep-copy operator = and drop
- 035 — operator: a growable string struct: nullable buffer, grow, join and drop
- 036 — operator: a struct owning heap buffers: deep-copy operator = and drop
- 037 — ref: ref return: create a node on the heap and return a reference to it
- 038 — ref: ref? return: one branch returns a reference, the other returns null
- 039 — ref: declare a ref and bind it to a ref-returning call
- 040 — ref: a ref-returning call passed straight into a ref parameter
- 041 — ref: a reference function must return a reference, not a bare value
- 042 — ref: a field read chained directly on a ref-returning call
- 043 — struct: a heap array of structs: new<Room>(N), then free
- 044 — struct: a 2D heap array of structs frees every inner slice
- 045 — struct: an unknown type as a parameter, a return type, or an array element
- 046 — struct: destructor must take no parameters
- 047 — struct: destructor must return void
- 048 — variable: global variables: scalars, arrays, forward reference, shadowing
- 049 — variable: a global struct is initialised and dropped like a local
- 050 — variable: a global is dropped on an early return too

---

## 001 — array: 2D array literal and nested indexing

```ura
// stdout: m[0][0]=1 m[1][1]=4
// stdout: m[0][1]=99
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    m i32[][] = [[1, 2], [3, 4]]
    io::out($"m[0][0]={m[0][0]} m[1][1]={m[1][1]}\n")
    m[0][1] = 99
    io::out($"m[0][1]={m[0][1]}\n")
    return 0
```

```llvm
@str.1 = private unnamed_addr constant [12 x i8] c"m[0][1]=%d\0A\00", align 1

define i8* @"new<i8>"(i32 %0) {
entry:
  %count = alloca i32, align 4
  store i32 %0, i32* %count, align 4
  %"$count" = load i32, i32* %count, align 4
  %MUL = mul i32 %"$count", ptrtoint (i8* getelementptr (i8, i8* null, i32 1) to i32)
  %AS = sext i32 %MUL to i64
  %call.calloc = call i8* @calloc(i64 %AS, i64 sext (i32 ptrtoint (i8* getelementptr (i8, i8* null, i32 1) to i32) to i64))
  ret i8* %call.calloc
}

define void @"copy<i8>"(i8* %0, i8* %1, i32 %2) {
entry:
  %dst = alloca i8*, align 8
  %src = alloca i8*, align 8
  %count = alloca i32, align 4
  store i8* %0, i8** %dst, align 8
  store i8* %1, i8** %src, align 8
  store i32 %2, i32* %count, align 4
  %"$dst" = load i8*, i8** %dst, align 8
  %"$src" = load i8*, i8** %src, align 8
  %"$count" = load i32, i32* %count, align 4
  %MUL = mul i32 %"$count", ptrtoint (i8* getelementptr (i8, i8* null, i32 1) to i32)
  %AS = sext i32 %MUL to i64
  %MUL1 = mul i64 %AS, sext (i32 ptrtoint (i8* getelementptr (i8, i8* null, i32 1) to i32) to i64)
  %call.memcpy = call i8* @memcpy(i8* %"$dst", i8* %"$src", i64 %MUL1)
  ret void
}

define void @"free<i8>"(i8* %0) {
entry:
  %data = alloca i8*, align 8
  store i8* %0, i8** %data, align 8
  %"$data" = load i8*, i8** %data, align 8
  call void @free(i8* %"$data")

}

declare i64 @write(i32, i8*, i64)

define void @"io::out"(%String %0) {
entry:
  %text = alloca %String, align 8
  store %String %0, %String* %text, align 8
  %call.String.chars = call i8* @String.chars(%String* %text)
  %call.String.len = call i32 @String.len(%String* %text)
  %AS = sext i32 %call.String.len to i64
  %call.write = call i64 @write(i32 1, i8* %call.String.chars, i64 %AS)
  call void @String.drop(%String* %text)
  ret void
}
```

## 002 — array: sized 2D matrix i32[N][M], zero-init and writes

```ura
// stdout: g[0][0]=1 g[2][3]=99 g[1][1]=0
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    g i32[][] = i32[3][4]
    g[0][0] = 1
    g[2][3] = 99
    io::out($"g[0][0]={g[0][0]} g[2][3]={g[2][3]} g[1][1]={g[1][1]}\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 003 — array: 3D sized array with runtime size

```ura
// stdout: v[1][1][1]=7 v[0][0][0]=0
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    n i32 = 2
    v i32[][][] = i32[n][n][n]
    v[1][1][1] = 7
    io::out($"v[1][1][1]={v[1][1][1]} v[0][0][0]={v[0][0][0]}\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 004 — array: heap array: new<i32>(N), zero-init, write, free

```ura
// stdout: a[0]=0 a[2]=42
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    a i32[] = new<i32>(5)
    a[2] = 42
    io::out($"a[0]={a[0]} a[2]={a[2]}\n")
    free<i32>(a)
    return 0
```

### llvm ir

```llvm
; ModuleID = 'ura'
source_filename = "ura"
target datalayout = "e-m:e-p270:32:32-p271:32:32-p272:64:64-i64:64-f80:128-n8:16:32:64-S128"
target triple = "x86_64-pc-linux-gnu"

%String = type { i8*, i32, i32 }

@str = private unnamed_addr constant [17 x i8] c"a[0]=%d a[2]=%d\0A\00", align 1

define i8* @"new<i8>"(i32 %0) {
entry:
  %count = alloca i32, align 4
  store i32 %0, i32* %count, align 4
  %"$count" = load i32, i32* %count, align 4
```

## 005 — array: heap array with a runtime size, new<i32>(n) + free

```ura
// stdout: a[5]=7 a[0]=0
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    n i32 = 3
    a i32[] = new<i32>(n * 2)
    a[5] = 7
    io::out($"a[5]={a[5]} a[0]={a[0]}\n")
    free<i32>(a)
    return 0
```

---

## 006 — array: multi-dim heap: new<i32[]>(N), a row each, free each

```ura
// stdout: m[0][0]=1 m[1][2]=9 m[0][1]=0
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    m i32[][] = new<i32[]>(2)
    m[0] = new<i32>(3)
    m[1] = new<i32>(3)
    m[0][0] = 1
    m[1][2] = 9
    io::out($"m[0][0]={m[0][0]} m[1][2]={m[1][2]} m[0][1]={m[0][1]}\n")
    free<i32>(m[0])
    free<i32>(m[1])
    free<i32[]>(m)
    return 0
```

## 008 — array: arr.len on a literal and on multi-dim

```ura
// stdout: len = 4
// stdout: rows = 3 cols = 4
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    nums i32[] = [10, 20, 30, 40]
    io::out($"len = {nums.len}\n")
    grid i32[][] = i32[3][4]
    io::out($"rows = {grid.len} cols = {grid[0].len}\n")
    return 0
```

## 012 — array: .len on a non-array

```ura
// error: '.len' is only valid on an array, not i32
fn main() i32:
    x i32 = 5
    io::out($"{x.len}")
    return 0
```

### stderr

```
error: test.ura:4:16 'x' is 'i32', not a struct
  |
4 |     io::out($"{x.len}")
  |                ^
error: aborting due to 1 error
```

### status

```
1
```

---

## 013 — array: array literals: one element, expressions, and strings

```ura
// stdout: 42 len 1
// stdout: 5 6 10 10
// stdout: ada grace alan len 3
// expect: 0

fn twice(n i32) i32:
    return n * 2

fn main() i32:
    // a single-element literal
    one i32[] = [42]
    io::out($"{one[0]} len {one.len}\n")

    // elements that are not compile-time constants
    x i32 = 5
    ex i32[] = [x, x + 1, x * 2, twice(x)]
    for v in ex:
        io::out($"{v} ")
    io::out("\n")

    // an array of strings
    names char[][] = ["ada", "grace", "alan"]
    for n in names:
        io::out($"{n} ")
    io::out($"len {names.len}\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 014 — array: fill a heap char buffer by index, print it, free it

```ura
// stdout: DUNGEON
// expect: 0

fn main() i32:
    str char[] = new<char>(8)
    str[0] = 'D'
    str[1] = 'U'
    str[2] = 'N'
    str[3] = 'G'
    str[4] = 'E'
    str[5] = 'O'
    str[6] = 'N'
    str[7] = 0 as char
    io::out($"{str}\n")
    free<char>(str)
    return 0
```

## 017 — heap: new<T>(1) gives one zeroed struct, a ref points at it

```ura
// stdout: 0 0
// stdout: 7
// expect: 0
struct P:
    a i32
    b i32

fn main() i32:
    ps P[] = new<P>(1)
    p &P = &ps[0]
    io::out($"{p.a} {p.b}\n")
    p.a = 7
    io::out($"{p.a}\n")
    free<P>(ps)
    return 0
```

---

## 021 — heap: operator drop frees the nodes a tree owns

```ura
// stdout: 1 2 3
// stdout: freed
// expect: 0
struct Node:
    value i32
    left &Node
    right &Node

struct Tree:
    nodes Node[]

    pub fn create() Tree:
        t Tree
        t.nodes = new<Node>(3)
        return t

    operator drop():
        free<Node>(self.nodes)
        io::out("freed\n")

fn main() i32:
    tree Tree = Tree::create()
    root &Node = &tree.nodes[0]
    root.value = 1
    root.left = &tree.nodes[1]
    root.left.value = 2
    root.right = &tree.nodes[2]
    root.right.value = 3
    io::out($"{root.value} {root.left.value} {root.right.value}\n")
    return 0
```

## 022 — heap: a function fills a heap struct and returns a ref to it

```ura
// stdout: 9
// expect: 0
struct P:
    v i32

fn make(x i32, ps P[]) &P:
    ps[0].v = x
    return &ps[0]

fn main() i32:
    ps P[] = new<P>(1)
    q &P = make(9, ps)
    io::out($"{q.v}\n")
    free<P>(ps)
    return 0
```

```ura
    a.x = 5
    io::out("done\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 031 — operator: a destructor fires on break and on continue

```ura
// stdout: drop 100
// stdout: drop 101
// stdout: after break
// stdout: drop 200
// stdout: drop 201
// stdout: after continue
// expect: 0

struct Tag:
    id i32

    pub fn create(n i32) Tag:
        t Tag
        t.id = n
        return t

    operator =(other &Tag) void:
        self.id = other.id

    operator drop():
        io::out($"drop {self.id}\n")

fn main() i32:
    i i32 = 0
    while i < 3:
        b Tag = Tag::create(100 + i)
        if i == 1:
            break
        i = i + 1
    io::out("after break\n")

    k i32 = 0
    while k < 2:
        c Tag = Tag::create(200 + k)
        k = k + 1
        continue
    io::out("after continue\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 032 — operator: drop on fall-through: an if block, each iteration, reverse order

```ura
// stdout: inside
// stdout: drop 1
// stdout: after if
// stdout: drop 10
// stdout: drop 11
// stdout: drop 12
// stdout: after loop
// stdout: end of main
// stdout: drop 8
// stdout: drop 7
// expect: 0

struct Tag:
    id i32

    pub fn create(n i32) Tag:
        t Tag
        t.id = n
        return t

    operator =(other &Tag) void:
        self.id = other.id

    operator drop():
        io::out($"drop {self.id}\n")

fn main() i32:
    if 1 < 2:
        x Tag = Tag::create(1)
        io::out("inside\n")
    io::out("after if\n")

    i i32 = 0
    while i < 3:
        y Tag = Tag::create(10 + i)
        i += 1
    io::out("after loop\n")

    p Tag = Tag::create(7)
    q Tag = Tag::create(8)
    io::out("end of main\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 033 — operator: a struct drops the structs it owns, at any depth, but not what it borrows

```ura
// stdout: built
// stdout: drop 1
// stdout: drop 3
// stdout: drop 9
// expect: 0

struct Res:
    id i32

    pub fn create(n i32) Res:
        r Res
        r.id = n
        return r

    operator =(other &Res) void:
        self.id = other.id

    operator drop():
        if self.id != 0:
            io::out($"drop {self.id}\n")

struct L3:
    res Res
struct L2:
    inner L3

// Owner declares no destructor of its own — everything below still drops
struct Owner:
    mid  L2
    also Res
    borrowed &Res

    pub fn create() Owner:
        o Owner
        o.mid.inner.res = Res::create(3)
        o.also          = Res::create(1)
        return o

fn main() i32:
    lent Res = Res::create(9)
    o Owner = Owner::create()
    o.borrowed = &lent
    io::out("built\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 034 — operator: a heap-owning struct: create, deep-copy operator = and drop

```ura
// stdout: b1: hello
// stdout: b2: world
// expect: 0

use "@/string"

struct Buffer:
    value char[]

    pub fn create() Buffer:
        b Buffer
        b.value = new<char>(64)
        return b

    operator drop():
        free<char>(self.value)

    operator = (s char[]) void:
        strcpy(self.value, s)

    operator = (b &Buffer) void:
        free<char>(self.value)
        self.value = new<char>(64)
        strcpy(self.value, b.value)

fn main() i32:
    b1 Buffer = Buffer::create()
    b1 = "hello"

    b2 Buffer = Buffer::create()
    b2 = b1

    strcpy(b2.value, "world")

    io::out($"b1: {b1.value}\n")
    io::out($"b2: {b2.value}\n")
    return 0
```

## 035 — operator: a growable string struct: nullable buffer, grow, join and drop

```ura
// stdout: s0:    1x2345 (6)
// stdout: s1:    6x789 (5)
// stdout: s0+s1: 1x23456x789 (11)
// expect: 0

use "@/header"

struct String2:
    value char[]
    count i32
    size  i32

    pub fn create() String2:
        s String2
        s.size  = 10
        s.value = new<char>(10)
        return s

    operator drop():
        free<char>(self.value)

    fn grow(want i32):
        if self.size >= want: return
        if self.size == 0: self.size = 10
        while self.size < want:
            self.size *= 2
        bigger char[] = new<char>(self.size)
        if self.count > 0: strcpy(bigger, self.value)
        free<char>(self.value)
        self.value = bigger

    fn assign(str char[]):
        len i32 = strlen(str) as i32
        self.grow(len + 1)
        bzero(self.value, self.size as i64)
        strcpy(self.value, str)
        self.count = len

    fn join(str char[]):
        len i32 = strlen(str) as i32
        self.grow(self.count + len + 1)
        strcat(self.value, str)
        self.count += len

    operator = (str char[]) void:
        self.assign(str)

    operator = (v &String2) void:
        self.assign(v.value)

fn add(v &String2, w &String2) String2:
    res String2 = String2::create()
    res = v
    res.join(w.value)
    return res

fn main() i32:
    s0 String2 = String2::create()
    s0 = "1x2345"

    s1 String2 = String2::create()
    s1 = "6x789"

    s2 String2 = String2::create()
    s2 = add(&s0, &s1)

    io::out($"s0:    {s0.value} ({s0.count})\n")
    io::out($"s1:    {s1.value} ({s1.count})\n")
    io::out($"s0+s1: {s2.value} ({s2.count})\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 036 — operator: a struct owning heap buffers: deep-copy operator = and drop

```ura
// stdout: The Bold Aldric enters!
// stdout: The Grim Borin enters!
// stdout: The Grim Borin enters!
// stdout: The Quick Cass enters!
// expect: 0
use "@/header"

struct Hero:
    name  char[]
    title char[]

    pub fn create() Hero:
        h Hero
        h.name  = new<char>(32)
        h.title = new<char>(32)
        return h

    fn setup(name char[], title char[]):
        strcpy(self.name,  name)
        strcpy(self.title, title)

    fn announce():
        io::out($"{self.title} {self.name} enters!\n")

    operator = (other &Hero) void:
        free<char>(self.name)
        free<char>(self.title)
        self.name  = new<char>(32)
        self.title = new<char>(32)
        strcpy(self.name,  other.name)
        strcpy(self.title, other.title)

    operator drop():
        free<char>(self.name)
        free<char>(self.title)

fn main() i32:
    hero Hero = Hero::create()
    hero.setup("Aldric", "The Bold")
    hero.announce()

    other Hero = Hero::create()
    other.setup("Borin", "The Grim")
    hero = other
    hero.announce()

    // the copy owns its own buffers, so mutating the source leaves it alone
    other.setup("Cass", "The Quick")
    hero.announce()
    other.announce()
    return 0
```

## 037 — ref: ref return: create a node on the heap and return a reference to it

```ura
// stdout: 42
// expect: 0
struct Node:
    value i32
    next &Node

fn make(v i32, heap Node[]) &Node:
    heap[0].value = v
    return &heap[0]

fn main() i32:
    h Node[] = new<Node>(1)
    a &Node = make(42, h)
    io::out($"{a.value}\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 038 — ref: ref? return: one branch returns a reference, the other returns null

```ura
// stdout: 7 True
// expect: 0
struct N:
    v i32
    nx &N

fn pick(h N[], want bool) &N:
    if want:
        return &h[0]
    return null

fn main() i32:
    h N[] = new<N>(1)
    h[0].v = 7
    r &N = pick(h, True)
    e &N = pick(h, False)
    io::out($"{r.v} {e == null}\n")
    return 0
```

## 040 — ref: a ref-returning call passed straight into a ref parameter

```ura

struct Node:
    v i32
    next &Node

struct Lst:

fn make(v i32, h Node[]) &Node:

    return &h[0]

fn main() i32:

    io::out($"{lst.head.v}\n")
    return 0
```

---

## 041 — ref: a reference function must return a reference, not a bare value

```ura
// error: 'g' returns a reference; return `ref x` (or null), not a value
struct N:
    v i32

fn g(h N[]) &N:
    return h[0]

fn main() i32:
    h N[] = new<N>(1)
    io::out($"{g(h).v}\n")
    return 0
```

### stderr

```
error: test.ura:6:13 expected '&N', found 'N'
  |
6 |     return h[0]
  |             ^
error: aborting due to 1 error
```

### status

```
1
```

---

## 042 — ref: a field read chained directly on a ref-returning call

```ura
// stdout: 42
// expect: 0
struct Node:
    value i32
    next &Node

fn make(v i32, heap Node[]) &Node:
    heap[0].value = v
    return &heap[0]

fn main() i32:
    h Node[] = new<Node>(1)
    io::out($"{make(42, h).value}\n")
    return 0
```

## 043 — struct: a heap array of structs: new<Room>(N), then free

```ura
// stdout: attic@9 2
// expect: 0

struct Room:
    name  char[]
    floor i32

fn main() i32:
    h Room[] = new<Room>(2)
    h[0].name  = "attic"
    h[0].floor = 9
    h[1].floor = 2
    io::out($"{h[0].name}@{h[0].floor} {h[1].floor}\n")
    free<Room>(h)
    return 0
```

## 044 — struct: a 2D heap array of structs frees every inner slice

```ura
// stdout: 1 4
// expect: 0

struct Room:
    floor i32

fn main() i32:
    g Room[][] = new<Room[]>(2)
    g[0] = new<Room>(2)
    g[1] = new<Room>(2)
    g[0][0].floor = 1
    g[1][1].floor = 4
    io::out($"{g[0][0].floor} {g[1][1].floor}\n")
    free<Room>(g[0])
    free<Room>(g[1])
    free<Room[]>(g)
    return 0
```

## 048 — variable: global variables: scalars, arrays, forward reference, shadowing

```ura
// stdout: ura 1.500000 True
// stdout: total starts 7
// stdout: total now 9
// stdout: xs 7 len 3  heap 9 len 2
// stdout: local total 99
// expect: 0

name  char[] = "ura"
ratio f32 = 1.5
flag  bool  = True
xs    i32[] = i32[3]
heap  i32[] = new<i32>(2)

fn touch():
    total += 1

fn main() i32:
    io::out($"{name} {ratio} {flag}\n")
    // forward reference: total is declared below main
    io::out($"total starts {total}\n")
    touch()
    touch()
    io::out($"total now {total}\n")

    xs[0] = 7
    heap[1] = 9
    io::out($"xs {xs[0]} len {xs.len}")
    io::out($"  heap {heap[1]} len {heap.len}\n")
    free<i32>(heap)

    // a local shadows the global for the rest of this scope
    total i32 = 99
    io::out($"local total {total}\n")
    return 0

total i32 = 7
```

---

## 049 — variable: a global struct is initialised and dropped like a local

```ura
// stdout: start 10
// stdout: after 12
// stdout: DROP at 12
// expect: 0

struct Counter:
    hits i32

    pub fn create(n i32) Counter:
        c Counter
        c.hits = n
        return c

    operator =(other &Counter) void:
        self.hits = other.hits

    fn bump():
        self.hits += 1

    operator drop():
        io::out($"DROP at {self.hits}\n")

tally Counter = Counter::create(10)

fn touch():
    tally.bump()

fn main() i32:
    io::out($"start {tally.hits}\n")
    touch()
    touch()
    io::out($"after {tally.hits}\n")
    return 0
```

## 050 — variable: a global is dropped on an early return too

```ura
// stdout: body
// stdout: early
// stdout: DROP 2
// stdout: DROP 1
// expect: 0

struct Res:
    id i32

    pub fn create(n i32) Res:
        r Res
        r.id = n
        return r

    operator =(other &Res) void:
        self.id = other.id

    operator drop():
        io::out($"DROP {self.id}\n")

a Res = Res::create(1)
b Res = Res::create(2)

fn main() i32:
    io::out("body\n")
    if 1 < 2:
        io::out("early\n")
        return 0
    io::out("unreachable\n")
    return 0
```
